Currently, Figure 1 is defined at the end of introduction.tex (lines 24-29), as a `figure*[t]` (full-width). It lands on page 3 because the introduction text fills page 1 and most of page 2. 

To get Figure 1 on page 2, I could:


1. Move the `\begin{figure*}[t]` to earlier in the introduction (before the contributions list)
2. Use `[t!]` to force top placement
3. Try `[p]` (float page)

The figure is already `[t]` which should place it at the top of the next available page, but the introduction text takes up pages 1-2, so the figure lands on page 3. My best bet is to move the figure definition to after the first paragraph of the introduction so LaTeX places it at the top of page 2.

But looking at page 2 more carefully, it's packed with the end of the introduction and the start of Related Work. Since the figure is full-width, moving it there would push other content down and potentially cascade into page overflow issues with the 8-page limit. Let me try placing it right after the first paragraph of the introduction and see if it fits without causing problems. I'm realizing the `figure*` environment placement is key here—in two-column layouts, it typically floats to the top of the next page after being defined. Moving it from the end of the introduction to right after the first paragraph should push it to an earlier page, which might help with the overall layout flow. Let me just make this change and see how it affects the page breaks.

    "oldString": "\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}",
    "newString": "",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \\citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces---as in neuro-symbolic approaches \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}---and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99\\% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \\citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer---a robust empirical regularity of zero-shot self-extraction pipelines we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is robust (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis reveals a deeper mechanism underlying these findings, providing a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTwenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \\citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence---a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the underlying mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\\footnote{Code and data will be released upon acceptance.}\n\n\\item \\textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n",

In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \citep{dietterich2000ensemble}.     "oldString": "Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \\citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces---as in neuro-symbolic approaches \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}---and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\looseness=-1",
    "newString": "Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \\citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces---as in neuro-symbolic approaches \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}---and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t!]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\looseness=-1",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \\citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces---as in neuro-symbolic approaches \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}---and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99\\% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \\citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer---a robust empirical regularity of zero-shot self-extraction pipelines we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is robust (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis reveals a deeper mechanism underlying these findings, providing a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTwenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \\citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence---a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the underlying mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\\footnote{Code and data will be released upon acceptance.}\n\n\\item \\textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n",

In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \\citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces---as in neuro-symbolic approaches \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}---and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. Now I need to clean up the PDF images and run through a consistency check, starting with verifying the abstract word count and making sure the experiments section is solid.

I'm noticing a potential terminology conflict: the table caption uses "confirmatory" to mean pre-registered for BH FDR correction (m=6), while the TOST paragraph uses it to mean conditions with MDE ≤ 2pp for equivalence testing (4 of 11). I should clarify whether the table caption needs updating to avoid this confusion. I see the tension — "confirmatory" means different things in the BH correction context (pre-registered for multiplicity adjustment) versus the TOST context (sufficient power to confirm equivalence). To avoid confusion, I could rephrase the TOST section to say "Four of 11 conditions have sufficient power to confirm equivalence" instead, keeping "confirmatory" only for the BH table caption where it's the standard terminology.

Looking at my abstract and intro, I've been using "confirm" as a verb rather than "confirmatory" as an adjective, which is cleaner. But in the experiments section I wrote "confirmatory" which creates that potential clash with the table caption. Let me change that to just "confirm equivalence" for consistency.

Now I need to verify the abstract stays under 200 words and check the word count on what I have so far. through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and over 1,200 instances, SICA reduces to majority voting: no condition shows significant improvement after FDR control; 4 of 11 conditions confirm equivalence (minimum detectable effect ≤ 2 percentage points (pp)), with the remaining 7 showing consistent directional evidence. We diagnose this as symbolic confirmation bias: under zero-shot self-extraction, constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer, with bias ratios 1.66--4.97× across nine models up to frontier scale. The mechanism is formalized through effective ensemble size K_eff from inter-trace agreement κ: correlated traces collapse nominal K=12 to K_eff as low as 1.20. But I find that twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies all fail to overcome this bottleneck. However, using an encoder-only verifier that's architecturally independent from the base model breaks through the bias, yielding a +6.67 pp improvement with strong statistical significance. I can also extract three zero-cost diagnostics that predict failure modes from just a single pilot run. tendency toward confirming the source model's answer, with bias ratios ranging from 1.66 to nearly 5× across nine models including frontier-scale ones. This confirmation bias stems from how the constraints are extracted—they systematically reinforce their originating trace rather than providing independent validation. I'm also measuring the effective ensemble size through inter-trace agreement, which shows how correlated predictions collapse the nominal ensemble of 12 down to as low as 1.20, explaining why the symbolic approach doesn't outperform simple majority voting.

Standard training-free fixes don't resolve this fundamental bottleneck, but switching to a cross-architecture encoder-only verifier breaks the bias pattern entirely, gaining 6.67 percentage points with strong statistical significance. This architectural independence restores the corrective signal that the same-model constraints couldn't provide. I've also identified three zero-cost diagnostics that can predict these failure modes from just a single pilot run, which should help practitioners catch this issue early.

Now I need to revise the experiments section to clarify the language around "confirmatory" so it doesn't get confused with the Benjamini-Hochberg correction terminology.

### Tool result

_source line 3063 · 2026-05-24T23:36:10.678Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01ETqeZTE8CBckv6ZLC6zDUB`</summary>

```text
12	
13	\looseness=-1
14	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99\% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation.
15	
16	\looseness=-1
17	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer---a robust empirical regularity of zero-shot self-extraction pipelines we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is robust (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis reveals a deeper mechanism underlying these findings, providing a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.
18	
19	\looseness=-1
20	Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\S\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence---a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
21	
22	Our contributions are:
23	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
```

24	\item \textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the underlying mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \S\ref{sec:remediation}).\footnote{Code and data will be released upon acceptance.}
25	
26	\item \textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
27	
28	\item \textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
29	\end{enumerate}
30	
31

    "oldString": "\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "newString": "\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a preliminary capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \\citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces---as in neuro-symbolic approaches \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}---and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t!]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99\\% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \\citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer---a robust empirical regularity of zero-shot self-extraction pipelines we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is robust (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis reveals a deeper mechanism underlying these findings, providing a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTwenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \\citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence---a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the underlying mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\\footnote{Code and data will be released upon acceptance.}\n\n\\item \\textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01UvnG7KrPdezxEcSsArTqbT`</summary>

```text
70	\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\geq 60\%$, BR $> 1$ $\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\leq 40\%$ $\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions (16 main, 27 remediation variants, 17 analytical) within the self-extraction paradigm.
71	
72	\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\Delta = -4.41$\,pp) and non-significance ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment.
```

```text
1	\looseness=-1 Our locally-run models range from 7B to 32B; frontier coverage relies on cross-model extraction, so we cannot confirm identical SCB patterns at frontier scale. All benchmarks are English logical reasoning; FOLIO and ProofWriter represent a best-case for symbolic verification, and the observed bias ratios ($1.66$--$4.97\times$) likely represent a \emph{lower bound} on SCB in less structured domains. Statistical power is limited for effects below ${\sim}2$\,pp (MDE $1.86$--$4.84$\,pp across conditions); small positive effects cannot be ruled out. We do not evaluate trained extractors, which could potentially break the self-extraction bound by introducing external signal. The cross-architecture escape (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) is validated with a single NLI architecture (DeBERTa); whether the capability-gap pattern generalizes to other verifier types (e.g., code executors, proof assistants) remains open. Our formal analysis assumes source-attribution scoring; alternative attribution schemes that decouple constraints from their originating traces could alter the degeneration condition, though no such scheme has been proposed.
2	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01C7UaBedEj2f8PNjz3YWmgP`</summary>

```text
19	\looseness=-1
20	Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\S\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence---a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
21

    "newString": "with benefit governed by a preliminary capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \\citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces---as in neuro-symbolic approaches \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}---and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t!]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99\\% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \\citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer---a robust empirical regularity of zero-shot self-extraction pipelines we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is robust (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis reveals a deeper mechanism underlying these findings, providing a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTwenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \\citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence---a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the underlying mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\\footnote{Code and data will be released upon acceptance.}\n\n\\item \\textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a preliminary capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Xbnbd75XGBqhBfCUibGeBY`</summary>

```text
1	\looseness=-1 Our locally-run models range from 7B to 32B; frontier coverage relies on cross-model extraction, so we cannot confirm identical SCB patterns at frontier scale. All benchmarks are English logical reasoning; FOLIO and ProofWriter represent a best-case for symbolic verification, and the observed bias ratios ($1.66$--$4.97\times$) likely represent a \emph{lower bound} on SCB in less structured domains. Statistical power is limited for effects below ${\sim}2$\,pp (MDE $1.86$--$4.84$\,pp across conditions); small positive effects cannot be ruled out. We do not evaluate trained extractors, which could potentially break the self-extraction bound by introducing external signal. The cross-architecture escape (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) is validated with a single NLI architecture (DeBERTa); whether the capability-gap pattern generalizes to other verifier types (e.g., code executors, proof assistants) remains open. Our formal analysis assumes source-attribution scoring; alternative attribution schemes that decouple constraints from their originating traces could alter the degeneration condition, though no such scheme has been proposed.
2	
```

</details>

_source line 3114 · 2026-05-24T23:38:21.024Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_016MZzgBZM543mUc2DEJ5NLy`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Self-Consistency (SC) improves large language model (LLM) reasoning via majority voting over chain-of-thought (CoT) traces.
3	A natural extension is to extract formal constraints and aggregate them with a symbolic solver---but does it work?
4	Under zero-shot extraction, we test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT.
5	Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and over 1{,}200 instances, SICA reduces to majority voting: no condition shows significant improvement after FDR control; 4 of 11 conditions confirm equivalence (minimum detectable effect $\leq 2$\,percentage points (pp)), with the remaining 7 showing consistent directional evidence.
6	We diagnose this as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: under zero-shot self-extraction, constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer, with bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models up to frontier scale.
7	The mechanism is formalized through effective ensemble size $K_\text{eff}$ from inter-trace agreement $\kappa$: correlated traces collapse nominal $K{=}12$ to $K_\text{eff}$ as low as 1.20.
8	Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck.
9	A cross-architecture encoder-only verifier escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), demonstrating that architectural independence restores corrective signal.
10	Three zero-cost diagnostics predict failure modes from a single pilot run.
11	\end{abstract}
12	
```

    "oldString": "A cross-architecture encoder-only verifier escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), demonstrating that architectural independence restores corrective signal.",
    "newString": "As preliminary evidence, a cross-architecture encoder-only verifier escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), suggesting that architectural independence can restore corrective signal.",
    "originalFile": "\\begin{abstract}\nSelf-Consistency (SC) improves large language model (LLM) reasoning via majority voting over chain-of-thought (CoT) traces.\nA natural extension is to extract formal constraints and aggregate them with a symbolic solver---but does it work?\nUnder zero-shot extraction, we test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and over 1{,}200 instances, SICA reduces to majority voting: no condition shows significant improvement after FDR control; 4 of 11 conditions confirm equivalence (minimum detectable effect $\\leq 2$\\,percentage points (pp)), with the remaining 7 showing consistent directional evidence.\nWe diagnose this as \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: under zero-shot self-extraction, constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer, with bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models up to frontier scale.\nThe mechanism is formalized through effective ensemble size $K_\\text{eff}$ from inter-trace agreement $\\kappa$: correlated traces collapse nominal $K{=}12$ to $K_\\text{eff}$ as low as 1.20.\nTwenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck.\nA cross-architecture encoder-only verifier escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), demonstrating that architectural independence restores corrective signal.\nThree zero-cost diagnostics predict failure modes from a single pilot run.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
    "structuredPatch": [

        "oldLines": 6,

          " Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck.",
          "-A cross-architecture encoder-only verifier escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), demonstrating that architectural independence restores corrective signal.",

    "originalFile": "\\looseness=-1 Our locally-run models range from 7B to 32B; frontier coverage relies on cross-model extraction, so we cannot confirm identical SCB patterns at frontier scale. All benchmarks are English logical reasoning; FOLIO and ProofWriter represent a best-case for symbolic verification, and the observed bias ratios ($1.66$--$4.97\\times$) likely represent a \\emph{lower bound} on SCB in less structured domains. Statistical power is limited for effects below ${\\sim}2$\\,pp (MDE $1.86$--$4.84$\\,pp across conditions); small positive effects cannot be ruled out. We do not evaluate trained extractors, which could potentially break the self-extraction bound by introducing external signal. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) is validated with a single NLI architecture (DeBERTa); whether the capability-gap pattern generalizes to other verifier types (e.g., code executors, proof assistants) remains open. Our formal analysis assumes source-attribution scoring; alternative attribution schemes that decouple constraints from their originating traces could alter the degeneration condition, though no such scheme has been proposed.\n",

  "content": "[Director 指令] [MF3 数据到位] 现在可以重构 Per-trace separation analysis 段落了。\n\n## 核心数据（Agent 分析结果）\n\nNon-unanimous problems: 166/204 (81.4%)\n\n**方向性分析结论**：\n- Source-attribution scoring 下，每条约束定义性地为其来源 trace 的答案投票 → support_own_trace = 100%\n- 公式级别分区：minority-exclusive 73.6%（1,911 unique formulas），cross-group shared 26.4%（687 formulas）\n- Per-trace density 均衡：majority 6.0 vs minority 5.9 constraints/trace\n- MAX-SAT exclusion 率相当：0.185 vs 0.172\n- Volume amplification ≈ 1.0×\n- 92.2% 的问题有跨组共享公式\n\n**论证结构**：SCB = pure volume effect。约束层忠实于各 trace（密度均衡、排除率相当），不引入额外偏差。共享公式（26.4%）在两组中都出现但各投各票 → 提供零区分信号。方向性完全由 source-attribution + trace 数量决定。\n\n## 重写要求\n\n替换 analysis.tex 中 \"Per-trace separation analysis\" 开头的段落（约 line 30-31 区域），用以下 3-test 结构重写：\n\n**结构**：\n1. **开头声明测试目标**：\"We directly test whether SCB is an independent bias introduced by the extraction function or a structural consequence of source-attribution scoring over correlated traces.\"\n2. **Test 1 (Extraction neutrality)**：balanced yield 6.0 vs 5.9, comparable MAX-SAT exclusion 0.185 vs 0.172 → extraction function f produces constraints of equal quality regardless of answer group\n3. **Test 2 (Formula undirectedness)**：73.6% minority-exclusive formulas exist, but 92.2% of problems contain cross-group shared formulas (26.4%) that provide no discriminative signal under source-attribution → constraints themselves are not semantically aligned with any particular answer\n4. **Test 3 (Source-attribution as sole mechanism)**：Under source-attribution, each constraint votes exclusively for its source trace's answer (100%). Volume amplification ≈ 1.0× → the constraint layer neither inflates nor dampens the CoT vote ratio. On 82.4% of problems where minority is correct, SICA still follows majority (correction rate 17.6%)\n5. **结论句**：\"SCB is therefore a transparency property: source-attribution scoring faithfully transmits the CoT trace distribution into the formal verification layer without adding or removing information. The structural novelty is identifying this transparency as the mechanism that renders formal verification vacuous—not a new cognitive bias but the algebraic consequence of scoring self-extracted constraints by their source.\"\n\n**约束**：\n- 保持 `\\looseness=-1` 前缀\n- 总长度不超过原段落 + 3 行（page budget）\n- 保留所有现有数据点，不丢失信息\n- 新增数据：73.6%/26.4% exclusive/shared split, \"100% support own trace\" (definitional under source-attribution)"
}

- Source-attribution scoring 下，每条约束定义性地为其来源 trace 的答案投票 → support_own_trace = 100%
- 公式级别分区：minority-exclusive 73.6%（1,911 unique formulas），cross-group shared 26.4%（687 formulas）
- Per-trace density 均衡：majority 6.0 vs minority 5.9 constraints/trace
- MAX-SAT exclusion 率相当：0.185 vs 0.172
- Volume amplification ≈ 1.0×
- 92.2% 的问题有跨组共享公式

**论证结构**：SCB = pure volume effect。约束层忠实于各 trace（密度均衡、排除率相当），不引入额外偏差。共享公式（26.4%）在两组中都出现但各投各票 → 提供零区分信号。方向性完全由 source-attribution + trace 数量决定。

## 重写要求

替换 analysis.tex 中 "Per-trace separation analysis" 开头的段落（约 line 30-31 区域），用以下 3-test 结构重写：

**结构**：
1. **开头声明测试目标**："We directly test whether SCB is an independent bias introduced by the extraction function or a structural consequence of source-attribution scoring over correlated traces."
2. **Test 1 (Extraction neutrality)**：balanced yield 6.0 vs 5.9, comparable MAX-SAT exclusion 0.185 vs 0.172 → extraction function f produces constraints of equal quality regardless of answer group
3. **Test 2 (Formula undirectedness)**：73.6% minority-exclusive formulas exist, but 92.2% of problems contain cross-group shared formulas (26.4%) that provide no discriminative signal under source-attribution → constraints themselves are not semantically aligned with any particular answer
4. **Test 3 (Source-attribution as sole mechanism)**：Under source-attribution, each constraint votes exclusively for its source trace's answer (100%). Volume amplification ≈ 1.0× → the constraint layer neither inflates nor dampens the CoT vote ratio. On 82.4% of problems where minority is correct, SICA still follows majority (correction rate 17.6%)
5. **结论句**："SCB is therefore a transparency property: source-attribution scoring faithfully transmits the CoT trace distribution into the formal verification layer without adding or removing information. The structural novelty is identifying this transparency as the mechanism that renders formal verification vacuous—not a new cognitive bias but the algebraic consequence of scoring self-extracted constraints by their source."

**约束**：
- 保持 `\looseness=-1` 前缀
- 总长度不超过原段落 + 3 行（page budget）
- 保留所有现有数据点，不丢失信息
- 新增数据：73.6%/26.4% exclusive/shared split, "100% support own trace" (definitional under source-attribution)

29	\smallskip\noindent
30	\textit{Per-trace separation analysis.}\;%
31	We test whether SCB is an independent bias introduced by the extraction function $f$, or a structural consequence of source-attribution scoring over correlated traces (Mistral-7B, FOLIO; 81\% of problems with non-unanimous votes).
32	\emph{Extraction neutrality:} per-trace constraint density is balanced between majority and minority answer groups ($6.0$ vs.\ $5.9$ constraints/trace) and MAX-SAT exclusion rates are comparable ($0.185$ vs.\ $0.172$)---$f$ is direction-agnostic.
33	\emph{Semantic undirectedness:} 92.2\% of non-unanimous problems contain cross-group shared formulas---the same constraint appears in both answer groups, carrying no directional information.
34	\emph{Source-attribution as sole mechanism:} volume amplification is ${\approx}1.0\times$---the constraint layer neither inflates nor dampens the CoT vote ratio---yet on 82.4\% of problems where the minority vote is correct, SICA follows the majority (correction rate 17.6\%), confirming that bias emerges exclusively from counting source-attributed constraints.
35	SCB is thus a transparency property: source-attribution scoring faithfully preserves the CoT trace distribution in the constraint layer, providing no corrective mechanism. The contribution is the structural identification of \emph{why} formal verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction, not a new type of bias.
36	
```
